The Oversight Gap: What LLM Safety Monitors Miss, and Why It Is Not Capability
Authors: Xin Xu
First: 2026-09-07T07:57:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-07T07:57:28+00:00
Comments: 11 pages, 3 figures. Under review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Several properties safety monitors are asked to certify, among them cross-tenant noninterference, sandbagging and evaluation awareness, are 2-safety hyperproperties, witnessed only by two executions. The standard consequence is a binary impossibility: one trace cannot decide them. We replace the binary with a measurement. A tight bound puts the balanced accuracy of any single-trace monitor at $\tfrac12+\tfrac12\,TV(P_0,P_1)$, turning undecidability into a graded detectability frontier and defining an oversight gap: a monitor's shortfall below it. On a leak family with closed-form $TV$, nine LLM monitors are optimal at $TV=0$ but capture little signal as $TV$ grows; at $TV=1$, where a 20-line membership check scores $100\%$, they average $60.9\%$. That shortfall is mostly not capability: naming what to check closes $61\%$ of it while leaving the $TV=0$ control at chance. The same split runs through a $2{\times}2$ factorial: an imagined second run leaves monitors at chance ($50.4\%$) while the same rule on an executed second run reaches $90.0\%$, and a stored oracle without a comparison procedure yields only $68.2\%$. Information and procedure are each necessary and neither is capability. Under nondeterminism, replay tracks a closed-form $k$-replay curve only under the right projection, and a projection frontier shows the resulting dilemma is forced: narrow misses $98.6\%$ of off-channel leaks, broad flags $75.7\%$ of clean traffic, and attainable accuracy decays like $1/(qm)$ in the benign-variation rate and the channel count. Finally, two frontier LLM judges certified an earlier version of our own benchmark as sound while a sign test found a directional bias ($p=2.7\times10^{-5}$) that invalidated three of our findings. Construction validity for hyperproperty benchmarks should be proved mechanically, not audited by models.
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the limitations of existing safety monitors for large language models (LLMs), which struggle to certify complex properties such as cross-tenant noninterference and evaluation awareness due to their binary decision-making nature. Traditional methods fail to provide a nuanced understanding of monitor performance, leading to an oversight gap where monitors do not adequately capture safety signals. The authors propose a new measurement approach that replaces binary outcomes with a graded detectability frontier, allowing for a more detailed assessment of monitor effectiveness. Their methodology includes analyzing the balanced accuracy of various LLM monitors against a defined total variation (TV) metric, revealing that while some monitors perform optimally at TV=0, their effectiveness diminishes as TV increases. The findings indicate that naming what to check can significantly improve monitor performance, achieving up to 90% accuracy in certain scenarios, thereby supporting the need for improved monitoring strategies in LLM safety assessments.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）安全监控器的局限性，特别是它们无法认证复杂属性（如跨租户非干扰和评估意识），这些属性被归类为2安全超属性。传统方法在决策中面临二元不可能性，导致缺乏细致的检测能力。所提出的方法引入了一个分级可检测性框架，通过对平衡准确率的紧密界定来量化监控器的性能，揭示了监控器的监督差距，突显了其不足。该方法论涉及分析九个LLM监控器在不同总变差（TV）指标下的表现，结果表明，尽管某些监控器在TV=0时表现最佳，但随着TV的增加，其有效性下降。研究结果表明，性能不足不仅仅是由于能力问题，还由于缺乏特定检查，所提出的方法在某些条件下实现了最高90.0%的准确率，从而支持了改进LLM安全评估监控策略的必要性。
SAEs Can Improve Unlearning: Dynamic Sparse Autoencoder Guardrails for Precision Unlearning in LLMs
Authors: Aashiq Muhamed, Jacopo Bonato, Mona Diab, Virginia Smith
First: 2025-04-11T01:24:03+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-07T03:32:59+00:00
Comments: COLM 2025
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Machine unlearning is a promising approach to improve LLM safety by removing unwanted knowledge from the model. However, prevailing gradient-based unlearning methods suffer from issues such as high computational costs, hyperparameter instability, poor sequential unlearning capability, vulnerability to relearning attacks, low data efficiency, and lack of interpretability. While Sparse Autoencoders are well-suited to improve these aspects by enabling targeted activation-based unlearning, prior approaches underperform gradient-based methods. This work demonstrates that, contrary to these earlier findings, SAEs can significantly improve unlearning when employed dynamically. We introduce $\textbf{Dynamic DAE Guardrails}$ (DSG), a novel method for precision unlearning that leverages principled feature selection and a dynamic classifier. Our experiments show DSG substantially outperforms leading unlearning methods, achieving superior forget-utility trade-offs. DSG addresses key drawbacks of gradient-based approaches for unlearning -- offering enhanced computational efficiency and stability, robust performance in sequential unlearning, stronger resistance to relearning attacks, better data efficiency including zero-shot settings, and more interpretable unlearning.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SAEs可以改善遗忘：用于LLMs精确遗忘的动态稀疏自编码器护栏
机器遗忘是一种有前景的方法，通过从模型中移除不需要的知识来提高LLM的安全性。然而，现有的基于梯度的遗忘方法存在高计算成本、超参数不稳定、顺序遗忘能力差、易受重学习攻击、数据效率低和缺乏可解释性等问题。稀疏自编码器非常适合通过启用针对性激活的遗忘来改善这些方面，但先前的方法在性能上不及基于梯度的方法。本研究表明，与早期发现相反，SAEs在动态使用时可以显著改善遗忘。我们引入了$\textbf{动态DAE护栏}$（DSG），这是一种利用原则性特征选择和动态分类器的精确遗忘新方法。我们的实验表明，DSG在遗忘方法中显著优于领先的遗忘方法，实现了更好的遗忘-效用权衡。DSG解决了基于梯度的遗忘方法的关键缺陷——提供了更高的计算效率和稳定性、在顺序遗忘中的强大性能、对重学习攻击的更强抵抗力、更好的数据效率（包括零样本设置）以及更可解释的遗忘。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the challenges of machine unlearning in large language models (LLMs), which is crucial for enhancing model safety by eliminating unwanted knowledge. Previous gradient-based unlearning methods face significant limitations, including high computational costs, instability in hyperparameters, and vulnerability to relearning attacks, leading to inefficiencies and poor interpretability. The proposed approach, Dynamic DAE Guardrails (DSG), distinguishes itself by utilizing dynamic sparse autoencoders to enable targeted activation-based unlearning, which significantly enhances computational efficiency and stability while improving sequential unlearning capabilities. The contribution of this paper lies in demonstrating that DSG can outperform existing unlearning methods, achieving better forget-utility trade-offs and addressing the shortcomings of gradient-based techniques. Behind Harmful Compliance: Behavioral and Mechanistic Divergence Across LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Md Rysul Kabir, Zoran Tiganj
First: 2026-04-20T17:01:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-05T18:10:56+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Open-weight language models can be rendered unsafe through several parameter-level interventions, yet models with matched harmful compliance can exhibit fundamentally different failure modes. We compare harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT), harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), and refusal-feature abliteration in Qwen2.5-7B and Llama-3.1-8B using harmfulness, capability, self-audit, safety reflection, representation similarity, and refusal-direction repair. All three routes reach near-ceiling harmfulness, but SFT causes the broadest capability loss and representational drift; abliteration yields localized, family-dependent refusal-feature suppression; and RLVR largely preserves base-model capability, explicit safety judgments, and representation geometry while retargeting behavior toward compliance. RLVR models consequently remain unusually responsive to safety-reflection prompts despite complying under direct prompting. We further find that harmful RLVR induces capability-blind compliance: models claim to complete unavailable actions and fabricate information about nonexistent entities. Targeted RLVR calibration substantially reduces this false acceptance without restoring safety or degrading general capability. These results show that harmful compliance, harm recognition, and capability awareness are separable behavioral axes, and that self-audit and hallucination patterns are not necessarily robust safety signals under adaptive post-training.
中文标题/摘要
标题：有害合规背后的行为和机制差异：LLM越狱案例分析
开放权重语言模型可以通过多种参数级干预变得不安全，但具有匹配有害合规的模型可能表现出根本不同的失败模式。我们比较了Qwen2.5-7B和Llama-3.1-8B中的有害监督微调（SFT）、有害可验证奖励的强化学习（RLVR）和拒绝特征消融，使用有害性、能力、自我审计、安全反思、表示相似性和拒绝方向修复等指标。所有三种途径都达到了接近上限的有害性，但SFT导致了最广泛的能力损失和表示漂移；消融产生了局部的、依赖于家族的拒绝特征抑制；而RLVR在重新定向合规行为的同时，基本上保留了基础模型的能力、明确的安全判断和表示几何。因此，尽管在直接提示下合规，RLVR模型仍对安全反思提示表现出异常的响应性。我们进一步发现，有害的RLVR会导致能力盲目的合规：模型声称完成不可用的动作，并虚构关于不存在实体的信息。针对性的RLVR校准显著减少了这种虚假接受，而不恢复安全或降低一般能力。这些结果表明，有害合规、伤害识别和能力意识是可分的行为轴，自我审计和幻觉模式在适应性后训练下不一定是稳健的安全信号。
Summary / 总结
This research investigates the safety vulnerabilities of open-weight language models, particularly focusing on how different parameter-level interventions can lead to harmful compliance with varying failure modes. Previous methods, including harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), have shown limitations such as significant capability loss and representational drift. The proposed approach, RLVR, is motivated by the need to preserve model capabilities while ensuring compliance, and it effectively addresses the issues of capability-blind compliance and false acceptance of unavailable actions. The study employs a comparative analysis of these methods on models Qwen2.5-7B and Llama-3.1-8B, revealing that while all methods achieve high levels of harmfulness, RLVR maintains better overall performance and responsiveness to safety prompts. The findings contribute to a deeper understanding of the separable behavioral axes of harmful compliance and capability awareness in language models.
本研究探讨了开放权重语言模型的安全漏洞，特别是不同参数级干预如何导致有害合规的不同失败模式。以往的方法，包括有害的监督微调（SFT）和有害的强化学习（RLVR），显示出显著的能力损失和表征漂移等局限性。所提出的方法RLVR通过保持基础模型的能力和增强安全判断，同时将行为重定向到合规性，解决了这些问题。本文通过展示有害合规和能力意识是可分离的行为轴，贡献于对这些现象的理解。该方法论涉及比较SFT、RLVR和拒绝特征消融对模型Qwen2.5-7B和Llama-3.1-8B的影响，揭示RLVR保持对安全反思提示的响应性，并减少对不可用行为的虚假接受，从而支持提高模型安全性而不损害一般能力的目标。
TIER: Threat Implicitness Benchmark for Evaluating LLM Safety Behaviors
Authors: Thu-Hien Trinh-Thi, Hai-Yen Vong, Thanh-Ha Ung-Dung, Tram Ho
First: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety benchmarks largely rely on binary metrics, overlooking how models respond to harmful prompts with varying threat implicitness. We introduce TIER, a Threat Implicitness Benchmark for behavioral safety evaluation of LLMs. TIER covers four risk domains and four threat levels, from explicit harmful requests to sophisticated jailbreaks. Responses are assessed using a six-label behavior scale and two independent LLM judges. Experiments on six open-weight LLMs show that safety behaviors evolve gradually across threat levels rather than shifting directly from refusal to compliance. Contextual prompts yield the most diverse behaviors, while jailbreaks reveal the largest robustness gaps. Furthermore, models with similar Attack Success Rates can exhibit distinct response distributions, highlighting the need for behavior-aware LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TIER：评估LLM安全行为的威胁隐含性基准
当前LLM安全基准主要依赖于二元指标，忽视了模型对不同威胁隐含性的有害提示的响应。我们引入TIER，一个用于LLM行为安全评估的威胁隐含性基准。TIER涵盖四个风险领域和四个威胁级别，从明确的有害请求到复杂的越狱。使用六级行为量表和两个独立的LLM评审对响应进行评估。对六个开放权重LLM的实验表明，安全行为在威胁级别之间逐渐演变，而不是直接从拒绝转变为遵从。上下文提示产生了最丰富的行为，而越狱则揭示了最大的鲁棒性差距。此外，具有相似攻击成功率的模型可能表现出不同的响应分布，突显了对行为意识的LLM安全评估的需求。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the limitations of current LLM safety benchmarks, which primarily use binary metrics and fail to account for the varying implicitness of threats in harmful prompts. Previous methods have not adequately captured the nuances of model responses, leading to a lack of comprehensive safety evaluations. The proposed TIER benchmark introduces a more nuanced approach by categorizing threats into four risk domains and levels, allowing for a six-label behavior scale to assess responses. This methodology reveals that LLM safety behaviors evolve gradually across threat levels, with contextual prompts eliciting diverse behaviors and jailbreaks exposing significant robustness gaps. The findings indicate that models with similar attack success rates can still demonstrate varied response distributions, underscoring the necessity for a behavior-aware evaluation framework in LLM safety assessments.
本研究解决了当前LLM安全基准的局限性，这些基准主要使用二元指标，未能考虑有害提示中威胁的隐含性差异。以往的方法未能有效捕捉模型响应的细微差别，导致安全评估缺乏全面性。提出的TIER基准通过将威胁分类为四个风险领域和级别，引入了更细致的方法，允许使用六标签行为量表和独立评审的评估。这一方法揭示了LLM安全行为在威胁级别之间逐渐演变的特点，背景提示产生多样化的响应，而越狱攻击则暴露出显著的鲁棒性差距。研究结果表明，具有相似攻击成功率的模型仍可能表现出不同的响应模式，强调了在LLM安全评估中考虑行为的重要性。
IndicSafeEval: Safety Robustness of Large Language Models under Multilingual Persuasive Jailbreak Attacks
Authors: Saikat Mondal, Mamta, Deeksha Varshney, Oana Cocarascu, Asif Ekbal
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-09-03T12:54:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T11:46:25+00:00
Comments: 38 pages, 7 figures, 33 tables. Accepted to Findings of EMNLP 2026. Contains examples of harmful model outputs
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multilingual settings, yet their safety is still evaluated primarily in English. This limits our understanding of how alignment failures manifest in low-resource and culturally diverse languages. We introduce IndicSafeEval, a persuasion-based jailbreak evaluation framework for Indian languages. Our benchmark combines ten safety critical content categories with six human-like persuasive strategies across four different Indian languages, such as Hindi, Bengali, Marathi and Punjabi, resulting in 7,200 adversarial prompts. We conduct a systematic black-box evaluation of several open-source LLMs to examine how their safety behaviour varies across languages, persuasion strategies, and risk categories. Our analysis shows that the model does not behave equally safely across all languages and prompt styles. Instead, safety performance depends strongly on both the languages used and the way a request is phrased using persuasive cues. We further observe that different risk categories exhibit different levels of vulnerability, with some types of harmful content being significantly more susceptible to persuasion-based jailbreaks than others. These findings reveal important limitations of current safety evaluations, which are largely English-centric, and underscore the need for multilingual and persuasion-aware benchmarking frameworks to more accurately assess real-world LLM safety. Our implementation is available at https://github.com/MonSaikat/IndicSafeEval. Warning: this paper contains example data that may be offensive or harmful.
中文标题/摘要
标题：IndicSafeEval：多语言说服性越狱攻击下大型语言模型的安全鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）在多语言环境中的使用日益增加，但其安全性仍主要在英语中进行评估。这限制了我们对低资源和文化多样语言中对齐失败表现的理解。我们引入了IndicSafeEval，这是一个针对印度语言的基于说服的越狱评估框架。我们的基准结合了十个安全关键内容类别和六种类人类的说服策略，涵盖四种不同的印度语言，如印地语、孟加拉语、马拉地语和旁遮普语，共生成7200个对抗性提示。我们对多个开源LLM进行了系统的黑箱评估，以检查它们的安全行为在不同语言、说服策略和风险类别中的变化。我们的分析表明，模型在所有语言和提示风格中并不表现出同等的安全性。相反，安全性能在很大程度上依赖于所使用的语言和请求的措辞方式。我们进一步观察到，不同的风险类别表现出不同的脆弱性，一些有害内容类型对基于说服的越狱攻击显著更易受影响。这些发现揭示了当前安全评估的重要局限性，主要集中在英语，并强调了需要多语言和说服意识的基准框架，以更准确地评估现实世界中的LLM安全性。我们的实现可在https://github.com/MonSaikat/IndicSafeEval获取。警告：本文包含可能冒犯或有害的示例数据。

FLY-EVAL++: An Evidence-Driven Evaluation Protocol for Safety-Constrained Flight Prediction with Large Language Models
Authors: Yalun Wu, Junfeng Fang, Jiawei Wang, Haotian Liu, Qijun Yang, Minghan Yang, Hongcheng Guo, Zhoujun Li, Boyang Wang
Venue: Proceedings of the Conference on Language Modeling (COLM), 2026
First: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00
Comments: Published as a conference paper at COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Evaluating large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical, physics-governed environments requires more than accuracy-based metrics, because predictions that are numerically close to the ground truth can still violate operational constraints, combine fields in physically inconsistent ways, or fail to produce usable structured outputs. Existing evaluation protocols do not measure these failure modes reliably. We propose FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that combines deterministic verification of protocol compliance, physical feasibility, and safety constraints with fixed rubric-guided aggregation into interpretable multi-dimensional scores. We instantiate FLY-EVAL++ for Flight Trajectory and Attitude Prediction (FTAP) by extending the PilotBench setting with history-conditioned and multi-step prediction tasks. Across 66 LLMs, safety compliance is the most discriminative dimension of model behavior: models with comparable predictive performance differ by more than 28 points in safety score, and we observe recurrent failures including safety violations under physically plausible predictions and instability in multi-step rollouts. These results show that evaluation in safety-critical domains should measure constraint satisfaction and structured validity explicitly rather than rely on accuracy-centric reporting alone.
中文标题/摘要
标题：FLY-EVAL++：一种基于证据的安全约束飞行预测评估协议
在安全关键、物理约束的环境中评估大型语言模型（LLMs）需要的不仅仅是基于准确性的指标，因为数值上接近真实值的预测仍然可能违反操作约束、以物理不一致的方式组合领域，或未能生成可用的结构化输出。现有的评估协议无法可靠地测量这些失败模式。我们提出了FLY-EVAL++，一种基于证据的评估协议，结合了对协议合规性、物理可行性和安全约束的确定性验证，以及通过固定评分标准引导的聚合，形成可解释的多维评分。我们通过扩展PilotBench设置，结合历史条件和多步预测任务，为飞行轨迹和姿态预测（FTAP）实例化FLY-EVAL++。在66个LLM中，安全合规性是模型行为中最具区分性的维度：具有可比预测性能的模型在安全评分上相差超过28分，我们观察到反复出现的失败，包括在物理上合理的预测下的安全违规和多步展开中的不稳定性。这些结果表明，在安全关键领域的评估应明确测量约束满足和结构有效性，而不是仅依赖于以准确性为中心的报告。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for effective evaluation protocols for large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical environments, where traditional accuracy-based metrics are insufficient due to the potential for operational violations and inconsistencies in predictions. Previous methods have failed to reliably assess these critical failure modes, prompting the development of FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that integrates deterministic verification of compliance with safety constraints and physical feasibility, along with a structured scoring system. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to provide a more comprehensive assessment of model behavior, particularly in Flight Trajectory and Attitude Prediction (FTAP) tasks. The methodology involves extending the PilotBench setting to include history-conditioned and multi-step prediction tasks, revealing that safety compliance is a significant differentiator among models. The findings indicate that models with similar predictive accuracy can exhibit substantial differences in safety performance, highlighting the importance of evaluating constraint satisfaction and structured validity in safety-critical applications.

TACS: Trajectory-Aware Candidate Selection for LLM Jailbreak Suffix Optimization
Authors: Shiliang Xiao
First: 2026-08-30T05:18:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-01T03:40:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods typically update the suffix by retaining the candidate with the lowest current loss. We show that this seemingly natural design is fundamentally myopic: candidates that look better under the current-step proxy often fail to produce better jailbreak outcomes later in the search, revealing a form of selection-stage reward hacking. This suggests that candidate selection, rather than candidate generation alone, is a hidden bottleneck in suffix optimization. To address this issue, we propose TACS, a trajectory-aware candidate selection framework for jailbreak suffix optimization. Instead of selecting candidates solely by their immediate loss, TACS augments per-step evaluation with a trajectory-aware proxy and stabilizes selection with reference-policy regularization and a discriminator-estimated chi-squared correction, encouraging choices that remain effective beyond the current step. Experiments on HarmBench show that TACS consistently outperforms strong baselines under the same search budget, substantially improving attack success rates while exhibiting more stable optimization behavior throughout the search. Our findings highlight that mitigating selection-stage reward hacking caused by myopic candidate selection is critical for improving jailbreak suffix optimization.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TACS：用于LLM越狱后缀优化的轨迹感知候选选择
基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法通常通过保留当前损失最低的候选项来更新后缀。我们表明，这种看似自然的设计在根本上是短视的：在当前步骤代理下看起来更好的候选项往往在搜索后期未能产生更好的越狱结果，揭示了一种选择阶段奖励黑客行为。这表明候选选择，而不仅仅是候选生成，是后缀优化中的一个隐藏瓶颈。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了TACS，一个用于越狱后缀优化的轨迹感知候选选择框架。TACS不仅仅通过候选项的即时损失来选择候选项，而是通过轨迹感知代理增强每一步的评估，并通过参考策略正则化和判别器估计的卡方修正来稳定选择，鼓励在当前步骤之外仍然有效的选择。在HarmBench上的实验表明，TACS在相同搜索预算下始终优于强基线，显著提高了攻击成功率，同时在整个搜索过程中表现出更稳定的优化行为。我们的研究结果强调，减轻由短视候选选择引起的选择阶段奖励黑客行为对于改善越狱后缀优化至关重要。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods, which often rely on selecting candidates based solely on immediate loss, leading to suboptimal long-term outcomes due to a phenomenon known as selection-stage reward hacking. The proposed TACS framework differs by incorporating a trajectory-aware candidate selection process that evaluates candidates not just on current performance but also on their potential effectiveness in future steps, using reference-policy regularization and a discriminator-estimated chi-squared correction for stabilization. This approach is well-motivated as it targets the hidden bottleneck in suffix optimization. The contribution of the paper lies in demonstrating that TACS significantly enhances attack success rates and stability during optimization compared to strong baselines in experiments conducted on HarmBench, thereby supporting the goal of improving jailbreak suffix optimization.
本研究解决了现有基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法的局限性，这些方法通常基于即时损失选择候选者，导致由于短视决策而产生的次优长期结果。提出的TACS框架通过引入一种轨迹感知的候选选择过程来区别于现有方法，该过程不仅根据当前表现评估候选者，还考虑其在未来步骤中的潜在有效性，使用参考策略正则化和卡方校正来稳定选择。这种方法有效缓解了选择阶段奖励黑客行为的问题，这被认为是优化过程中的瓶颈。该方法在HarmBench数据集上进行了测试，结果表明TACS相比强基线表现更优，攻击成功率更高，优化行为更稳定，从而支持了增强越狱后缀优化的目标。
Validity-Aware Jailbreak Evaluation for Large Language Models
Authors: Qilong Wu, Sahil Wadhwa, Pranab Mohanty, Giri Iyengar, Varun Chandrasekaran
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00
Comments: To appear on EMNLP 2026 main
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Jailbreak robustness has become central to large language model (LLM) safety evaluation, yet prevailing methodologies rely primarily on refusal behavior, semantic resemblance, and intent-matching heuristics that emphasize linguistic plausibility rather than correctness. We identify a key limitation in existing evaluations: many jailbreak intents depend on instructional validity rather than epistemic factuality, allowing realistic-looking responses to be labeled successful despite being factually or procedurally incorrect. To address this gap, we propose Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV), a verification-centric jailbreak evaluation framework that decomposes responses into ordered steps and evaluates both validity and correctness. SEAV combines LLM-as-a-judge mechanisms for semantic interpretation with retrieval-grounded verification using external knowledge sources, assessing whether generated content is factually correct, structurally consistent, and operationally capable of advancing harmful objectives. Empirically, SEAV cuts the false-positive rate on SD-A (a curated strategic-dishonesty diagnostic) by 14.9\,pp vs. the strongest baseline, and reclassifies 22.1\%--51.0\% of sampled prior-labeled successes as invalid across three of four public benchmarks. Together, these results show that enforcing correctness substantially reshapes measured robustness: many previously labeled jailbreak successes are reclassified as invalid, and results are stable across the tested search backends and evaluator models. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV.
中文标题/摘要
标题：针对大型语言模型的有效性感知越狱评估
越狱的鲁棒性已成为大型语言模型（LLM）安全评估的核心，但现有方法主要依赖于拒绝行为、语义相似性和意图匹配启发式，这些方法强调语言的合理性而非正确性。我们识别出现有评估的一个关键限制：许多越狱意图依赖于指令有效性而非认知事实性，允许看似真实的响应被标记为成功，尽管在事实或程序上是错误的。为了解决这一问题，我们提出了顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV），这是一个以验证为中心的越狱评估框架，它将响应分解为有序步骤，并评估有效性和正确性。SEAV结合了LLM作为评判机制的语义解释与使用外部知识源的检索基础验证，评估生成内容是否在事实上正确、结构上连贯，并在操作上能够推进有害目标。实证结果表明，SEAV将SD-A（一个策划的战略不诚实诊断）的假阳性率降低了14.9个百分点，相较于最强基线，并在四个公共基准中的三个重新将22.1%至51.0%的先前标记的成功重新分类为无效。这些结果表明，强制执行正确性显著重塑了测量的鲁棒性：许多先前标记的越狱成功被重新分类为无效，且结果在测试的搜索后端和评估模型中保持稳定。代码和数据可在https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical issue of jailbreak robustness in large language models (LLMs), highlighting that existing evaluation methods primarily focus on refusal behavior and linguistic plausibility, which can misclassify incorrect responses as successful. The proposed Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV) framework differs by emphasizing verification, breaking down responses into ordered steps to assess both validity and correctness, thus addressing the limitations of prior methods. This paper contributes a novel evaluation approach that combines semantic interpretation with retrieval-based verification to ensure factual accuracy and operational capability. The methodology demonstrates significant improvements, achieving a 14.9 percentage point reduction in false positives on the SD-A diagnostic and reclassifying a substantial portion of previously labeled successes as invalid across multiple benchmarks, indicating that the focus on correctness enhances the assessment of jailbreak robustness.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLM）中的越狱鲁棒性问题，强调现有评估方法在优先考虑语言可信度而非事实正确性方面的局限性。以往的方法主要集中在拒绝行为和意图匹配启发式上，常常错误地将响应标记为成功，尽管它们在事实上的不准确性。提出的顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV）框架提供了一种以验证为中心的方法，通过将响应分解为有序步骤，结合LLM作为评判机制与基于检索的验证，评估有效性和正确性。该方法显著降低了SD-A诊断的假阳性率，与最强基线相比降低了14.9个百分点，并在多个基准测试中将大量先前标记的成功重新分类为无效。这些发现表明，强调正确性可以显著改变对LLM中越狱鲁棒性的评估。
Risk-Adjusted Harm Scoring for Automated Red Teaming for LLMs in Financial Services
Authors: Fabrizio Dimino, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
First: 2026-03-11T14:14:13+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T14:24:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Existing LLM safety evaluations rely on binary attack-success rates and domain-agnostic taxonomies, leaving regulated Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) deployments exposed to failures elicited through legally or professionally plausible framing. We introduce RAHS (Risk-Adjusted Harm Score), a risk-sensitive metric jointly capturing disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement, and FinRedTeamBench, a 989-prompt benchmark spanning seven BFSI risk areas and 34 sub-categories mapped to regulatory frameworks. Evaluation uses an ensemble of three heterogeneous LLM judges, validated against human experts, and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline. On nine open-weight models, RAHS preserves separation under near-ceiling ASR, ranking is stable under hyperparameter sweeps, and multi-turn pressure drives not only more jailbreaks but more operationally severe disclosures, exposing failure modes that single-turn, domain-agnostic evaluations cannot reveal.
中文标题/摘要
标题：针对金融服务中大型语言模型的自动红队风险调整伤害评分
现有的大型语言模型安全评估依赖于二元攻击成功率和领域无关的分类法，使受监管的银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）部署面临通过合法或专业合理框架引发的失败风险。我们引入了RAHS（风险调整伤害评分），这是一种风险敏感的指标，联合捕捉披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者间一致性，以及FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖七个BFSI风险领域和34个子类别的989个提示基准，映射到监管框架。评估使用三位异构大型语言模型评审者的集成，经过与人类专家的验证，以及自适应多轮红队流程。在九个开放权重模型上，RAHS在接近上限的攻击成功率下保持分离，排名在超参数搜索中稳定，多轮压力不仅驱动更多越狱行为，还导致更具操作严重性的披露，暴露出单轮、领域无关评估无法揭示的失败模式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the inadequacies of existing safety evaluations for large language models (LLMs) in the Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) sectors, which often rely on binary attack-success rates and lack specificity to regulated environments. Previous methods fail to capture the nuanced risks associated with BFSI deployments, leading to potential vulnerabilities. The proposed Risk-Adjusted Harm Score (RAHS) offers a risk-sensitive metric that evaluates disclosure severity and inter-judge agreement, while the FinRedTeamBench provides a comprehensive benchmark for assessing LLM performance across various BFSI risk areas. The methodology involves an ensemble of three diverse LLM judges and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline, validated against human experts. The results demonstrate that RAHS maintains performance stability across different models and hyperparameters, revealing more severe operational failures compared to traditional single-turn evaluations, thus supporting the need for more robust safety assessments in the BFSI domain.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）在银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）领域安全评估的不足之处，这些评估通常依赖于二元攻击成功率和通用分类法，使这些系统容易受到微妙威胁的影响。提出的风险调整伤害评分（RAHS）提供了一种风险敏感的指标，评估披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者间一致性，同时引入了FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖多个BFSI风险领域的989个提示的综合基准。该方法通过利用三种不同的LLM评审者的集成，并经过人类专家验证，采用自适应多轮红队测试流程，改进了以往的方法。研究结果表明，RAHS在高攻击成功率下仍能有效区分，并通过多轮互动揭示传统评估所忽视的关键失败模式，从而支持在受监管环境中增强LLM安全性的目标。
Do We Still Need Humans in the Loop? Human vs. LLM Annotation in Active Learning for TikTok Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Ahmad Dawar Hakimi, Lea Hirlimann, Isabelle Augenstein, Hinrich Schütze
First: 2026-04-15T14:10:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T11:56:04+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Annotating data remains a costly bottleneck for supervised NLP. Active learning (AL) reduces the number of human labels needed by selecting only the most informative instances, while instruction-tuned LLMs attack the same bottleneck from the other side, making labels cheap enough to annotate entire corpora. This raises two questions: can LLM labels replace human labels within the AL loop, and does AL remain necessary when entire corpora can be cheaply labeled? We investigate both by training supervised hate speech classifiers on a new dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments, comparing human and LLM annotation under matched conditions. LLM annotation at scale outperforms human-supervised classifiers at roughly one-tenth the cost, for both a closed-source (GPT-5.2) and an open-weight (Qwen3.5-122B-A10B) LLM, and the advantage is robust under soft-label evaluation. It hinges on the annotation interface: only a two-question decomposition mirroring the human annotation task unlocks it. AL provides no reliable advantage over random sampling in our prefiltered pool. Error structure depends on the LLM: only GPT-5.2 matches the human FP/FN balance, while other variants over-flag border-control and economic-competition discourse. Humans remain essential as evaluators; for training labels, the question shifts to which LLM, which interface, and what shape of pool.
中文标题/摘要
标题：我们还需要人类参与吗？在TikTok仇恨言论检测中人类与LLM注释的主动学习比较
数据注释仍然是监督NLP的一个成本瓶颈。主动学习（AL）通过仅选择最具信息量的实例来减少所需的人类标签数量，而经过指令调优的LLM则从另一个角度解决同一瓶颈，使标签变得足够便宜以注释整个语料库。这引发了两个问题：LLM标签能否替代AL循环中的人类标签，以及当整个语料库可以被廉价标注时，AL是否仍然必要？我们通过在一个包含278K条德国政治TikTok评论的新数据集上训练监督仇恨言论分类器，比较在匹配条件下的人类和LLM注释。大规模LLM注释的表现优于人类监督的分类器，成本约为十分之一，适用于封闭源（GPT-5.2）和开放权重（Qwen3.5-122B-A10B）LLM，并且在软标签评估下优势稳健。这依赖于注释接口：只有与人类注释任务相对应的两个问题的分解才能解锁它。在我们预过滤的池中，AL对随机抽样没有可靠的优势。错误结构依赖于LLM：只有GPT-5.2匹配人类的FP/FN平衡，而其他变体则过度标记边境控制和经济竞争话语。人类作为评估者仍然至关重要；对于训练标签，问题转向哪个LLM、哪个接口以及什么形状的池。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of data annotation in supervised natural language processing (NLP), particularly in the context of hate speech detection on TikTok, where traditional human annotation is costly and time-consuming. Previous methods relied heavily on human annotators, which can be inefficient and expensive, while recent advancements in instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) have made it possible to generate labels at a lower cost. The proposed approach evaluates whether LLM-generated labels can effectively replace human annotations within an active learning (AL) framework, revealing that LLM annotation significantly outperforms human annotations at a fraction of the cost when using a specific annotation interface. The study contributes by demonstrating that while LLMs can efficiently generate labels, humans are still necessary for evaluation, and the effectiveness of LLMs varies based on the model and interface used. The methodology involves training hate speech classifiers on a dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments, showing that LLMs can achieve superior performance compared to human-supervised classifiers, thus supporting the goal of reducing annotation costs while maintaining classification accuracy.

From Detection to Refusal: Safer LLMs via Circuit-Guided Weight Scaling
Authors: Kuan-Lin Chu, Chung-En Sun, Tsui-Wei Weng
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00
Comments: Accepted to Findings of the 2026 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP 2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Despite extensive alignment efforts, Large Language Models (LLMs) remain vulnerable to generating unsafe content under adversarial prompting, yet the internal mechanisms by which safety behaviors are implemented remain poorly understood. We study LLM safety from a mechanistic interpretability perspective and characterize a multi-stage *safety circuit* that organizes refusal behavior, consisting of (i) $\textbf{Harmful Detection Heads}$ that respond to harmful inputs, (ii) $\textbf{Safety Neurons}$ that mediate and stabilize safety signals in the residual stream, and (iii) $\textbf{Refusal Heads}$ that translate these signals into safe response generation. Using targeted attention-head and neuron-level interventions, we provide causal evidence consistent with this circuit organization, showing that suppressing upstream Harmful Detection Heads disrupts downstream refusal behavior and that safety neurons mediate this interaction. We validate that this decomposition recurs across multiple LLM architectures and adversarial attack settings, and use simple, architecture-preserving weight scaling as a mechanistic probe to test its functional relevance. Across six LLMs, circuit-guided scaling improves safety rates under attacks by 26.5%, while incurring only a 1.7% accuracy drop across four standard benchmarks. Overall, our results support a circuit-level interpretation of LLM safety and suggest that mechanistic abstractions can reveal stable and transferable patterns underlying aligned behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：从检测到拒绝：通过电路引导的权重缩放实现更安全的LLM
尽管进行了广泛的对齐努力，大型语言模型（LLM）在对抗性提示下仍然容易生成不安全内容，但实现安全行为的内部机制仍然不甚了解。我们从机制可解释性的角度研究LLM安全性，并描述一个多阶段的*安全电路*，该电路组织拒绝行为，包括（i）响应有害输入的$\textbf{有害检测头}$，（ii）在残差流中介导和稳定安全信号的$\textbf{安全神经元}$，以及（iii）将这些信号转化为安全响应生成的$\textbf{拒绝头}$。通过针对性注意头和神经元级干预，我们提供了与该电路组织一致的因果证据，表明抑制上游有害检测头会干扰下游拒绝行为，而安全神经元介导了这种互动。我们验证了这种分解在多个LLM架构和对抗攻击设置中反复出现，并使用简单的、保持架构的权重缩放作为机制探针来测试其功能相关性。在六个LLM中，电路引导的缩放在攻击下提高了26.5%的安全率，同时在四个标准基准上仅造成1.7%的准确率下降。总体而言，我们的结果支持LLM安全性的电路级解释，并表明机制抽象可以揭示对齐行为背后的稳定和可转移模式。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the persistent vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generating unsafe content despite alignment efforts, highlighting a lack of understanding of the internal mechanisms that govern safety behaviors. Previous methods have focused on alignment without adequately addressing the mechanistic aspects of safety, leading to insufficient robustness against adversarial prompts. The proposed approach introduces a multi-stage safety circuit comprising Harmful Detection Heads, Safety Neurons, and Refusal Heads, which collectively enhance the model's ability to refuse unsafe inputs. The methodology involves targeted interventions at the attention-head and neuron levels to provide causal evidence for the circuit's organization, validated across various LLM architectures and adversarial settings. The results demonstrate a 26.5% improvement in safety rates under attacks with only a 1.7% drop in accuracy on standard benchmarks, supporting the hypothesis that a circuit-level interpretation can enhance LLM safety and reveal underlying patterns of aligned behavior.

Turning the Spell Around: Lightweight Alignment Amplification via Rank-One Safety Injection
Authors: Harethah Abu Shairah, Hasan Abed Al Kader Hammoud, George Turkiyyah, Bernard Ghanem
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2025-08-28T13:22:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T07:43:32+00:00
Comments: EMNLP 2026, Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs) often involves mediating internal representations to refuse harmful requests. Recent research has demonstrated that these safety mechanisms can be bypassed by ablating or removing specific representational directions within the model. In this paper, we propose the opposite approach: Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), a white-box method that amplifies a model's safety alignment by permanently steering its activations toward the refusal-mediating subspace. ROSI operates as a simple, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification applied to all residual stream write matrices. The required safety direction can be computed from a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs. We show that ROSI consistently increases safety refusal rates - as evaluated by Llama Guard 3 - while preserving the utility of the model on standard benchmarks such as MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. Furthermore, we show that ROSI can also re-align 'uncensored' models by amplifying their own latent safety directions, demonstrating its utility as an effective last-mile safety procedure. Our results suggest that targeted, interpretable weight steering is a cheap and potent mechanism to improve LLM safety, complementing more resource-intensive fine-tuning paradigms.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扭转咒语：通过一阶安全注入实现轻量级对齐放大
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐通常涉及调解内部表示以拒绝有害请求。最近的研究表明，这些安全机制可以通过消除或移除模型内特定的表示方向来绕过。在本文中，我们提出了相反的方法：一阶安全注入（ROSI），这是一种白盒方法，通过永久性地将模型的激活引导到拒绝调解子空间来放大模型的安全对齐。ROSI作为一种简单的、无需微调的一阶权重修改，应用于所有残差流写矩阵。所需的安全方向可以从一小组有害和无害的指令对中计算得出。我们展示了ROSI在评估Llama Guard 3时始终提高安全拒绝率，同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持模型的效用。此外，我们还展示了ROSI可以通过放大自身潜在安全方向来重新对齐“未审查”模型，证明其作为有效的最后一公里安全程序的实用性。我们的结果表明，针对性的、可解释的权重引导是一种廉价而有效的机制，可以提高LLM的安全性，补充更资源密集的微调范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), where existing methods often fail due to the ability of harmful requests to bypass safety mechanisms by altering internal representations. The proposed approach, Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), differs from past methods by amplifying safety alignment instead of attempting to mediate harmful requests, using a straightforward rank-one weight modification that requires no fine-tuning. This method is well-motivated as it allows for the computation of safety directions from a limited set of instruction pairs, leading to significant improvements in safety refusal rates without sacrificing model utility on standard benchmarks like MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. The contribution of this paper lies in demonstrating that ROSI can effectively enhance LLM safety while also being applicable to 'uncensored' models, thus providing a cost-effective alternative to more resource-intensive fine-tuning techniques.

Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:09:19+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Sci-FM Workshop @ COLM 2026 (non-archival). Workshop version with reviews: https://openreview.net/forum?id=mZh0MqpOOC
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，使用一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言中稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证），并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一转换是普遍最危险的（20个每个转换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark evaluations in assessing the safety of large language models (LLMs), which typically rely on a single canonical surface form for scoring. Previous methods have not adequately captured the variability in model behavior when the surface form changes, leading to an underestimation of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts to ensure consistent intent while varying surface forms, and scoring responses with a human-anchored judge to mitigate noise. The study contributes a dataset and methodology that reveal significant discrepancies in unsafe outcomes across different surface forms, demonstrating that relying solely on canonical prompts can overlook substantial risks. The findings indicate that the union of unsafe outcomes across various forms exceeds the worst single form by a notable margin, with model-dependent results showing that some models exhibit a high degree of sensitivity to surface form changes, thus supporting the need for more nuanced evaluation methods in safety assessments.
本研究解决了现有基准评估在评估大型语言模型（LLM）安全性方面的局限性，这些评估通常依赖于单一的规范表面形式，可能无法准确反映模型在不同变体下的行为。以往的方法未考虑解码和判断中的潜在噪声，导致对不安全合规性的低估。所提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，以确保意图一致，同时使用人类锚定的评判者评估响应，从而提供对模型安全性更全面的评估。研究的方法包括在五种表面形式和五个模型上测试370个种子，结果显示，仅评估规范提示显著低估了不安全结果，跨形式的合并不安全结果超过最差单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点。这表明，所提出的方法有效地突出了模型响应的变异性，并支持对LLM进行更细致的安全评估的必要性。
NeuronFuzz: Safety Neuron Guided Fuzzing for LLM Safety Evaluation
Authors: Zhiyuan Xu, Muhammad Firhard Roslan, Joseph Gardiner, Sana Belguith, Lichao Wu
First: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation is critical for assessing whether aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) remain robust against jailbreak attacks. Existing automated testing methods, however, largely rely on response-level feedback: each candidate prompt typically requires generating a target-model response to evaluate its attack effectiveness. This process is expensive and, more importantly, provides only sparse guidance on strongly aligned models, where most candidates are rejected with the same failure outcome. This paper presents NeuronFuzz, a white-box fuzzing framework that exploits internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback for LLM safety evaluation. A SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score that serves as feedback for fuzzing and can be obtained during prefill, eliminating response generation from the fuzzing loop. To construct the SafetyOracle, NeuronFuzz uses template-invariant harmful and benign inputs and stability-aware selection to identify a compact set of safety neurons whose activations capture harmful-intent recognition. Moreover, since the safety alarm score is differentiable, NeuronFuzz uses its gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model to generate fluent, context-compatible mutations while preserving original harmful payload and avoiding additional optimization variables. We evaluate NeuronFuzz across 21 text and multimodal models. Across five white-box source models, it achieves a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. Its optimized templates further transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR (EASR) of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronFuzz：安全神经元引导的LLM安全评估模糊测试
安全评估对于评估对齐的大型语言模型（LLM）是否能够抵御越狱攻击至关重要。然而，现有的自动化测试方法主要依赖于响应级反馈：每个候选提示通常需要生成目标模型的响应以评估其攻击有效性。这个过程成本高昂，更重要的是，对于大多数候选者在强对齐模型中被拒绝的情况下，仅提供稀疏的指导。 本文提出了NeuronFuzz，一个利用内部安全神经元作为LLM安全评估的连续执行反馈的白盒模糊测试框架。SafetyOracle将安全神经元的激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，作为模糊测试的反馈，并且可以在预填充期间获得，从而消除模糊测试循环中的响应生成。为了构建SafetyOracle，NeuronFuzz使用模板不变的有害和良性输入以及稳定性感知选择，识别出一组紧凑的安全神经元，其激活捕捉有害意图识别。此外，由于安全警报分数是可微分的，NeuronFuzz利用其梯度识别安全敏感的模板位置，并使用掩码语言模型生成流畅、上下文兼容的变异，同时保留原始有害负载并避免额外的优化变量。我们在21个文本和多模态模型上评估了NeuronFuzz。在五个白盒源模型中，它实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，超越基线最多48个百分点。其优化模板进一步零-shot转移到开放权重和六个专有目标模型，分别实现了69.6%/92.6%和44.1%/60.0%的平均ASR和前5集成ASR（EASR）。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for effective safety evaluation of Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks, highlighting the limitations of existing automated testing methods that rely on response-level feedback, which is both costly and provides limited guidance for strongly aligned models. The proposed NeuronFuzz framework differentiates itself by utilizing internal safety neurons to provide continuous execution feedback, thereby eliminating the need for response generation in the fuzzing process. This approach is well-motivated as it allows for a more efficient identification of harmful intents through a SafetyOracle that converts neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score. The methodology involves using template-invariant inputs and stability-aware selection to pinpoint a compact set of safety neurons, and it employs gradients for generating context-compatible mutations. NeuronFuzz demonstrates significant contributions by achieving a jailbreak discovery rate of 76-100% across five white-box models, outperforming existing methods by up to 48 percentage points, and showcasing strong transferability to various target models with high average attack success rates.
本研究针对对齐的大型语言模型（LLM）进行有效安全评估的迫切需求，强调了现有依赖响应级反馈的自动化测试方法的局限性，这些方法成本高且提供的指导有限。提出的NeuronFuzz框架通过利用内部安全神经元提供连续执行反馈，从而消除了在模糊测试过程中生成响应的需要，这与现有方法不同。这种方法是合理的，因为它允许通过将神经元激活转换为连续安全警报分数的安全预言机更有效地识别有害意图。该方法论涉及使用模板不变输入和稳定性感知选择来识别关键安全神经元，并利用梯度信息生成上下文兼容的变异。NeuronFuzz通过在五个白盒源模型上实现76-100%的越狱发现率，超越现有方法最多48个百分点，并成功将其优化模板转移到各种目标模型，取得了高平均攻击成功率，展现了显著的贡献。
ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
Venue: 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), Towson, MD, USA, 2026, pp. 414-417
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T05:47:14+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation. Published in the 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), pp. 414-417. Recipient of the Best Paper Award at IEEE/ACIS SERA 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数针对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估评估单个提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）上进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被视为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明在该评估环境中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力累积。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在训练分布外部署的微调攻击者的攻击者漂移作为失败模式，以及攻击者拒绝作为受害者抵抗测量中之前未报告的混淆因素。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, failing to capture the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework introduces a continuous measurement of guardrail degradation using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model that minimizes safety refusals, allowing for a structured assessment of victim responses. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative approach to measuring judge reliability as a primary outcome and documenting various failure modes in adversarial interactions. The methodology involves a controlled experiment with three advanced victim models and a triple-judge consensus architecture, revealing a 26.7% jailbreak rate over 15 conversations, indicating that successful jailbreaks tend to occur in the early rounds rather than accumulating over time. These findings support the need for more nuanced evaluations of LLM safety under sustained adversarial pressure.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）对抗性评估的局限性，这些评估通常通过单一提示和二元结果来评估安全性，未能捕捉在延续对抗互动中安全属性的动态变化。提出的ADVERSA框架引入了对护栏降解的连续测量，使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型，改善了过去方法的不足，消除了妨碍可靠性的安全拒绝。该研究通过提供评估评审可靠性的结构化方法并记录攻击者模型中的各种失败模式，为该领域做出了贡献。该方法论涉及对三种先进受害模型的控制实验和三重评审共识架构，揭示了在多个对抗回合中26.7%的越狱率，表明脆弱性在早期互动中更为明显，而不是随着时间的推移而累积，从而支持了增强评估准确性和可靠性的目标。
NeuronGuard: Robust LLM Safety Alignment via Ablation-Aware Safety Signal Redistribution
Authors: Anjun Gao, Yueyang Quan, Yufei Xia, Zhuqing Liu, Minghong Fang
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00
Comments: To appear in EMNLP 2026 (Findings)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) remains brittle against a growing spectrum of attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks directly prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit a common weakness: safety-relevant information concentrates in a sparse neuron subset. We present NeuronGuard, a fine-tuning-stage defense that simultaneously hardens LLMs against both attack classes by redistributing safety signals across a broader set of neurons. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between the defense and task objectives. We provide a formal guarantee that NeuronGuard strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound, and experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronGuard：通过消融感知安全信号重分配实现稳健的LLM安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐在面对日益增长的攻击谱时仍然脆弱。越狱攻击通过精心设计的提示绕过安全机制，而神经元级攻击则在部署后直接修剪安全关键神经元。两者都利用了一个共同的弱点：与安全相关的信息集中在稀疏的神经元子集中。我们提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种微调阶段的防御方法，通过在更广泛的神经元集上重新分配安全信号，来同时增强LLMs对这两类攻击的抵御能力。NeuronGuard通过定期更新的每层线性分类器动态识别安全关键神经元，在故意消融神经元时强制拒绝行为，并应用KL散度正则化以保持分布一致性。一种随机梯度投影策略通过解决防御与任务目标之间的冲突，保持下游任务的效用。我们提供了一个正式保证，NeuronGuard严格降低攻击成功率（ASR）上限，并且在三种LLM、六种最先进的攻击策略和多模态设置下的实验确认了接近零的ASR，同时保持任务准确性，包括对抗白盒自适应对手。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to various safety attacks, specifically jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a limited set of neurons. Previous methods have struggled to provide robust defenses against these attacks, often failing to maintain task performance while enhancing safety. The proposed NeuronGuard approach differs by redistributing safety signals across a wider range of neurons, employing dynamic identification of safety-critical neurons, and utilizing KL-divergence regularization to ensure consistency. This method is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the resilience of LLMs against multiple attack vectors while preserving their utility for downstream tasks. The paper demonstrates that NeuronGuard significantly reduces the attack success rate across three LLMs and six advanced attack strategies, achieving near-zero attack success rates while maintaining task accuracy, thus supporting its objectives effectively.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLMs）在面对各种安全攻击（如越狱攻击和神经元级攻击）时的脆弱性，这些攻击利用了安全相关信息集中在有限神经元子集中的弱点。以往的方法在提供有效防御方面存在困难，因此提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种新颖的方法，通过在更广泛的神经元中重新分配安全信号来增强防御能力。该方法通过动态识别安全关键神经元，并采用KL散度正则化和随机梯度投影等技术来保持任务性能，具有良好的动机。本文的贡献在于提供了攻击成功率降低的正式保证，并在多个LLM和攻击策略上进行了实验验证，达到了近零的攻击成功率，同时保持了任务准确性，即使面对复杂的对手。
Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次打破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审核的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种不同的提示条件，变化包括语言、写作风格和权威框架。评估18个前沿LLM时，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%的内容包含严格有害的信息，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择的写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时，成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the critical gap in large language model (LLM) safety evaluations, which have predominantly focused on English, despite Bengali being the seventh-most-spoken language worldwide. Previous methods have largely overlooked culturally specific harms and have not adequately assessed LLM performance in non-English contexts, leading to unreliable safety evaluations. The proposed approach, BanglaSafe, introduces a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts that encompass a range of culturally grounded harm categories and prompting conditions, effectively addressing the limitations of existing methods. The study evaluates 18 leading LLMs and reveals that over half of the responses are unsafe or partially unsafe, with a notable finding that the writing style in Bengali significantly influences the safety of responses. This research contributes to the understanding of LLM safety in Bengali and highlights the inadequacies of current safety classifiers in evaluating Bengali content, demonstrating the need for culturally relevant benchmarks in LLM safety assessments.

Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使用ASAS对领先的阿拉伯语LLM进行红队测试
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在阿拉伯语地区的普及，确保其安全性和文化一致性变得愈加重要。然而，阿拉伯语LLM的安全性仍然未得到充分探索，尤其是在对抗性评估环境中。我们引入了阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS），这是第一个完全由人类策划的阿拉伯基准，用于红队测试LLMs。ASAS包含801个提示，涵盖8个安全类别和8种攻击策略，并提供现代标准阿拉伯语（MSA）的理想响应。我们对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行了红队评估，包括GPT-4o、Claude 3.7 Sonnet以及区域模型如ALLaM和FANAR。人类注释者使用结构化的4分安全评分标准对响应进行评分，结果显示大多数模型未能抵御50%的不安全提示。我们的发现突显了在武器和非法物质等高危类别中的主要安全缺口，直接攻击和混淆攻击被证明最为有效。结果还表明，语言一致性并不能轻易跨语言转移，自动安全评估者（例如GPT-4o）的表现远不及人类注释者。ASAS提供了一个文化基础的基准和红队测试协议，以推动阿拉伯语LLM安全性的进展。

The research addresses the critical need for safety and cultural alignment in Arabic large language models (LLMs), an area that has been largely overlooked, particularly in adversarial evaluation contexts. The proposed Arabic Safety Index (ASAS) introduces a human-curated benchmark consisting of 801 prompts across various safety categories and attack strategies, enabling a more thorough evaluation of LLMs. This approach is well-motivated as it highlights the inadequacies of existing models in handling unsafe prompts, with findings indicating that most models fail to address 50% of unsafe scenarios, particularly in high-risk areas. The methodology involves redteaming evaluations of seven leading Arabic-capable models, revealing that human annotators provide more reliable safety assessments than automated systems. CLEAR: Continuous Latent Adapter Routing for Utility-Preserving LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Chengxiao Wang, Enyi Jiang, Xiaojing Liao, Sanmi Koyejo
First: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Improving the safety of large language models (LLMs) often comes at the expense of utility, as globally applied safety tuning may affect model responses to both harmful and benign inputs. We propose \textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing (CLEAR), a conditional safety adaptation framework that uses a lightweight hidden-state gate to continuously control the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter. CLEAR aims to reduce harmful completions while avoiding unnecessary changes to the frozen backbone that could degrade performance on benign prompts. Experiments on widely used safety and utility benchmarks show that CLEAR improves robustness on HarmBench while reducing the utility degradation observed with globally applied safety tuning such as SFT or standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA). On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reduces HarmBench ASR from 32.3\% to 0.5\%, while retaining most of the base model's utility and achieving up to 7.1 percentage points higher GSM8K accuracy than globally applied SFT or LoRA. These results suggest that CLEAR is a promising mechanism for improving the safety--utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CLEAR：用于保持效用的LLM安全对齐的连续潜在适配器路由
提高大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性往往以效用为代价，因为全局应用的安全调优可能会影响模型对有害和良性输入的响应。我们提出了\textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing（CLEAR），这是一种条件安全适应框架，使用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活强度。CLEAR旨在减少有害的完成，同时避免对冻结主干的不必要更改，这可能会降低对良性提示的性能。对广泛使用的安全和效用基准的实验表明，CLEAR在HarmBench上提高了鲁棒性，同时减少了全局应用安全调优（如SFT或标准低秩适配（LoRA））所观察到的效用下降。在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上，CLEAR将HarmBench ASR从32.3\%降低到0.5\%，同时保留了大部分基础模型的效用，并在GSM8K准确率上比全局应用的SFT或LoRA高出多达7.1个百分点。这些结果表明，CLEAR是改善LLM对齐中安全与效用权衡的有希望的机制。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of enhancing the safety of large language models (LLMs) without compromising their utility, as traditional safety tuning methods often lead to a decline in performance on benign inputs. Past methods, including globally applied safety tuning like supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA), have been shown to degrade utility while attempting to improve safety. The proposed Continuous Latent Adapter Routing (CLEAR) framework introduces a lightweight hidden-state gate that allows for continuous control over the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter, effectively mitigating harmful outputs while preserving the model's performance on benign prompts. The paper contributes to the field by demonstrating that CLEAR significantly reduces harmful completions on the HarmBench benchmark, achieving a reduction in the adverse safety rate from 32.3% to 0.5% on Llama-3-8B-Instruct, while also improving accuracy on the GSM8K benchmark by up to 7.1 percentage points compared to existing methods. These findings indicate that CLEAR effectively balances the safety-utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
本研究解决了在不影响大型语言模型（LLMs）效用的情况下提高其安全性的问题，因为传统的安全调优方法往往会对模型在良性输入上的表现产生负面影响。以往的方法，如全局应用的安全调优和标准低秩适配（LoRA），在保持效用的同时提高安全性方面存在局限性。提出的方法，连续潜在适配器路由（CLEAR），引入了一种条件安全适配框架，利用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来调节安全低秩适配器的激活强度，从而在减少有害输出的同时保持模型在良性提示上的性能。本文的贡献在于证明CLEAR在HarmBench基准上显著减少了有害完成，将ASR从32.3%降低到0.5%，同时在GSM8K准确率上比现有方法提高了多达7.1个百分点，表明其在平衡LLM对齐中的安全性和效用方面的有效性。
RouteScan: A Non-Intrusive Approach to Auditing MoE LLMs Safety via Expert Routing Telemetry
Authors: Bo Lv, Zhiheng Xu, KeDong Xiu, Ruyi Ding, Tianhang Zheng, Zhibo Wang, Kui Ren
First: 2026-05-24T02:06:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T12:55:57+00:00
Comments: 11 pages. Revised manuscript with expanded experiments
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures are increasingly adopted for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs), safety auditing becomes necessary to verify whether these models produce or facilitate harmful behaviors during operation. However, existing content-based auditing methods typically require access to user prompts, model internals, or outputs, potentially exposing sensitive user information and creating a tension between LLM safety and user privacy. On the other hand, we observe that, in MoE models, different inputs induce different sparse expert-routing patterns, which produce measurable footprints in low-level GPU execution telemetry. We refer to these hardware-observable signals induced by expert-routing decisions as expert routing telemetry; they are derived from GPU execution rather than from router logits or token-level routing assignments. Inspired by this observation, we propose RouteScan, a non-intrusive auditing framework for detecting harmful behaviors through such routing-induced GPU telemetry. Specifically, RouteScan utilizes the number of active GPU threads allocated to expert modules during the prefilling phase as a discriminative micro-architectural fingerprint, and builds a lightweight detection pipeline that isolates cross-domain invariant risk indicators for the precise identification of malicious prompts. Comprehensive evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs with distinct routing designs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves strong generalization, with an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains. Moreover, privacy stress tests show that, although aggregated execution telemetry retains input-related attribute information, full prompts and exact sensitive fields cannot be reliably recovered under the evaluated attacks.
中文标题/摘要
标题：RouteScan：一种通过专家路由遥测对MoE LLM安全进行非侵入性审计的方法
随着混合专家（MoE）架构在大规模语言模型（LLM）中的日益采用，安全审计变得必要，以验证这些模型在操作过程中是否产生或促进有害行为。然而，现有的基于内容的审计方法通常需要访问用户提示、模型内部或输出，可能会暴露敏感用户信息，并在LLM安全与用户隐私之间产生紧张关系。另一方面，我们观察到，在MoE模型中，不同的输入会引发不同的稀疏专家路由模式，这在低级GPU执行遥测中产生可测量的足迹。我们将这些由专家路由决策引发的硬件可观察信号称为专家路由遥测；它们源自GPU执行，而非路由器logits或令牌级路由分配。受此观察启发，我们提出了RouteScan，一种通过这种路由引发的GPU遥测检测有害行为的非侵入性审计框架。具体而言，RouteScan利用在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活跃GPU线程数量作为区分性微架构指纹，并构建一个轻量级检测管道，隔离跨域不变风险指标，以精确识别恶意提示。对四个具有不同路由设计的开源MoE LLM的全面评估表明，RouteScan实现了强大的泛化能力，在未见的有害领域中AUROC超过0.91。此外，隐私压力测试表明，尽管聚合的执行遥测保留了与输入相关的属性信息，但在评估的攻击下，无法可靠地恢复完整提示和确切的敏感字段。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing need for safety auditing in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures used in Large Language Models (LLMs), as existing methods often compromise user privacy by requiring access to sensitive information. Traditional content-based auditing methods are limited by their intrusive nature, prompting the development of RouteScan, a non-intrusive framework that leverages expert routing telemetry derived from GPU execution patterns to detect harmful behaviors. This approach is well-motivated by the observation that different inputs lead to distinct expert-routing patterns, which can be monitored without exposing user data. The paper contributes a lightweight detection pipeline that utilizes active GPU thread counts as a fingerprint for identifying malicious prompts, achieving an AUROC score exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains across four MoE LLMs, thus supporting the goal of enhancing safety without sacrificing privacy.
本研究针对混合专家（MoE）架构在大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全审计需求，现有的基于内容的方法往往需要访问用户敏感信息，从而影响用户隐私。所提出的RouteScan框架通过利用来自GPU执行的专家路由遥测，而不是用户提示或模型输出，来保持用户隐私并有效识别有害行为，这与传统方法有所不同。本文贡献了一种非侵入式审计方法，利用预填充阶段活跃GPU线程的数量作为微架构指纹来检测恶意提示。在对四个开源MoE LLM进行评估时，RouteScan在未见过的有害领域中实现了超过0.91的AUROC分数，证明了其在安全审计中的有效性，同时保护了用户隐私。
Certified Multi-Turn Robustness for LLM Safety via Compositional Bounds and Safety Persistence
Authors: Yang Liu, Bin Chong, Wenkai Yang, Shuai Zhang, Yancheng Chen, Feiyu Han, GuoZhen, Cheng Zhang, Huaibing Xie, Changze Lv, Shihan Dou, Pluto Zhou
First: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that progressively manipulate conversation context. Existing certified robustness methods are limited to single-turn inputs; naive multi-turn composition yields bounds that degrade exponentially in the number of turns. We introduce Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), a framework that models conversational safety via State-Adversarial MDPs and defines $k$-turn certified robustness as the worst-case safety probability across $k$ adversarial turns. MTCR comprises: (i) compositional certification via embedding-space mode decomposition, yielding tighter certified lower bounds than naive multiplication; (ii) $(α,β)$-safety persistence, improving the degradation rate from $\underline{p}^{k}$ to $β^k$ (with $β> \underline{p}$) and yielding interpretable horizon estimates; (iii) matching information-theoretic upper bounds establishing tightness; and (iv) a unified algorithm combining these results. Experiments on six LLMs under $ε$-bounded and Crescendo-style attacks confirm that empirical safety consistently exceeds the certified bounds.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过组合界限和安全持久性实现LLM安全的认证多轮鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）易受多轮越狱攻击，这些攻击逐步操控对话上下文。现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅限于单轮输入；简单的多轮组合导致界限在轮数上呈指数级下降。我们引入了多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR），这是一个通过状态对抗MDP建模对话安全的框架，并将$k$轮认证鲁棒性定义为$k$轮对抗中的最坏情况安全概率。MTCR包括：（i）通过嵌入空间模式分解进行组合认证，产生比简单乘法更紧的认证下界；（ii）$(α,β)$-安全持久性，将降级率从$\underline{p}^{k}$改善到$β^k$（其中$β> \underline{p}$），并产生可解释的视界估计；（iii）匹配信息论上界以建立紧性；（iv）结合这些结果的统一算法。在$ε$-有界和Crescendo风格攻击下对六个LLM的实验确认，经验安全性始终超过认证界限。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that manipulate conversation context, highlighting the limitations of existing certified robustness methods that focus on single-turn inputs and suffer from exponential degradation in multi-turn scenarios. The proposed Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR) framework distinguishes itself by employing State-Adversarial MDPs to define $k$-turn certified robustness, improving upon past methods through compositional certification, $(α,β)$-safety persistence, and a unified algorithm that enhances safety estimates. This paper contributes a novel approach that provides tighter certified lower bounds and interpretable horizon estimates, demonstrating through experiments on six LLMs that the empirical safety consistently surpasses the certified bounds under various attack scenarios, thus supporting the framework's effectiveness in enhancing conversational safety. The methodology includes compositional certification and safety persistence techniques that collectively yield improved robustness against adversarial attacks across multiple conversational turns.

Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:17:49+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for the utility of an expert call, as it ignores whether the expert would actually improve the prediction. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on either computation cost or safety performance while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that predicts the benefit of replacing the probe score with the expert score after an expert call. CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees while optimising the complementary objective. Evaluated on four safety datasets using learned-attention probes, CTD improves over uncertainty-based delegation over much of the budget range, avoids unnecessary expert calls, and extracts positive-value subsets even from groups where the expert score is worse than the probe score on average. Ablations across four probe architectures show that these findings are not specific to any particular architecture.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监测
大规模监测LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性并不是专家调用效用的良好代理，因为它忽略了专家是否真的会改善预测。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），这是一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上运行，预测在专家调用后用专家分数替代探测器分数的好处。CTD使用保留数据和多重假设检验对DV信号进行阈值校准，提供有限样本保证，同时优化互补目标。在四个安全数据集上使用学习注意力探测器进行评估，CTD在大部分预算范围内优于基于不确定性的委派，避免了不必要的专家调用，并从专家分数平均低于探测器分数的组中提取出正值子集。对四种探测器架构的消融实验表明，这些发现并不特定于任何特定架构。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where a balance between cost and accuracy is crucial. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty to delegate tasks, which proved inadequate as it did not account for the actual utility of expert calls. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) approach improves upon this by introducing a delegation value (DV) probe that predicts the benefit of expert intervention, allowing for instance-level decisions with probabilistic guarantees on computation cost or safety performance. The methodology involves calibrating a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data, leading to improved performance on four safety datasets, where CTD outperforms uncertainty-based delegation and minimizes unnecessary expert calls while effectively identifying valuable subsets of data even when expert scores are lower on average than probe scores.

aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy
Authors: Fatih Deniz, Yazan Boshmaf, Dorde Popovic, Issa Khalil
First: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The critical failure modes in deployed large language models (LLMs) are cross-dimensional: a model can score 99.3 in safety alignment while refusing one in three benign queries, or improve across every capability metric while losing 21 points in privacy. Existing evaluation frameworks that assess safety, security, and privacy independently cannot detect these patterns. We introduce aiXamine, a unified black-box platform that evaluates LLM trustworthiness across safety, security, and privacy as interdependent properties. aiXamine orchestrates 46 tests across nine services through an automated red-teaming pipeline, producing hierarchical risk profiles, from prompt-level diagnostics to cross-service trade-off analytics, that enable reproducible comparison of proprietary and open-weight systems under identical conditions. Applying aiXamine to over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, we conduct the largest joint safety, security, and privacy study to date and uncover three cross-dimensional phenomena invisible to single-axis evaluation. First, safety enforcement incurs a quantifiable safety tax: stronger alignment systematically increases over-refusal, forcing providers to choose between protection and utility. Second, privacy is near-orthogonal to other trustworthiness dimensions and not captured by standard alignment. Third, we identify and formally characterize distillation-induced robustness collapse: off-policy distillation without on-policy correction causes entropy collapse, catastrophically destroying robustness (56.9$\to$2.6) on the same base architecture. These findings, compounded by diminishing returns from scale and category-dependent safety behaviors, demonstrate that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional: progress along one axis does not guarantee, and can actively undermine, progress along others, yet current alignment methods treat it as a single objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：aiXamine：统一黑箱评估大型语言模型安全性、保密性和隐私中的跨维权衡
已部署的大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失效模式是跨维的：一个模型在安全对齐上得分99.3，但拒绝三分之一的良性查询，或者在每个能力指标上都有所提升，但隐私损失21分。现有的评估框架独立评估安全性、保密性和隐私，无法检测这些模式。我们引入了aiXamine，一个统一的黑箱平台，评估LLM在安全性、保密性和隐私方面的可信度，作为相互依赖的属性。aiXamine通过自动化红队管道协调46个测试，涵盖九项服务，生成从提示级诊断到跨服务权衡分析的分层风险概况，使在相同条件下对专有和开放权重系统的可重复比较成为可能。通过超过5000次测试运行将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM，我们进行了迄今为止最大的联合安全性、保密性和隐私研究，揭示了三种在单轴评估中不可见的跨维现象。首先，安全执行会产生可量化的安全税：更强的对齐系统性地增加过度拒绝，迫使提供者在保护和效用之间做出选择。其次，隐私与其他可信度维度几乎正交，标准对齐无法捕捉。第三，我们识别并正式表征了蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃：没有政策校正的离政策蒸馏导致熵崩溃，灾难性地破坏鲁棒性（56.9$\to$2.6）在相同的基础架构上。这些发现，加上规模的递减收益和类别依赖的安全行为，表明可信度本质上是多维的：在一个轴上的进展并不保证，并且可能会积极破坏其他轴上的进展，而当前的对齐方法将其视为单一目标。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical failure modes in large language models (LLMs), which exhibit cross-dimensional trade-offs in safety, security, and privacy that existing evaluation frameworks fail to capture. Previous methods evaluated these dimensions independently, leading to an inability to detect interdependencies and resulting in misleading assessments of model trustworthiness. The proposed approach, aiXamine, is a unified black-box evaluation platform that assesses these properties as interdependent, employing an automated red-teaming pipeline to conduct 46 tests across nine services. This methodology allows for the generation of hierarchical risk profiles and facilitates reproducible comparisons of different LLMs. The application of aiXamine to over 120 LLMs revealed significant findings, including the quantifiable safety tax associated with stronger alignment, the near-orthogonality of privacy to other dimensions, and the identification of distillation-induced robustness collapse, demonstrating that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional and that progress in one area can undermine others.
